00034| ## Abstract  摘要
00035| 
00036| Solid [polymer electrolytes](https://www.sciencedirect.com/topics/materials-science/polymer-electrolyte "Learn more about polymer electrolytes from ScienceDirect's AI-generated Topic Pages") are the most promising [solid electrolytes](https://www.sciencedirect.com/topics/materials-science/solid-electrolyte "Learn more about solid electrolytes from ScienceDirect's AI-generated Topic Pages") due to their mechanical flexibility and manufacturing scalability. However, the low lithium-ion transference number and [battery](https://www.sciencedirect.com/topics/engineering/battery-electrochemical-energy-engineering "Learn more about battery from ScienceDirect's AI-generated Topic Pages") failure with detrimental dendrites growth inhibit its commercial application in solid-state batteries. Here amorphous [silicon nitride](https://www.sciencedirect.com/topics/chemical-engineering/silicon-nitride "Learn more about silicon nitride from ScienceDirect's AI-generated Topic Pages") with high permittivity was introduced to both restrain the anion motion and screen the electric potential under [external electric field](https://www.sciencedirect.com/topics/engineering/external-electric-field "Learn more about external electric field from ScienceDirect's AI-generated Topic Pages"), by which the lithium-ion transference number was improved and the dendrite growth was inhibited significantly. The symmetric Li//Li cell paired with this solid polymer electrolyte exhibits a high lithium-ion transference number of 0.53, with excellent lithium plating/stripping capability at [high current density](https://www.sciencedirect.com/topics/engineering/high-current-density "Learn more about high current density from ScienceDirect's AI-generated Topic Pages") of 1.0 mA cm−2 over 250 h at room temperature. The practical application of this solid polymer electrolyte is verified by the capacity retention of 86.5% over 500 cycles and 70.5% even after 1000 cycles at room temperature with Li//LiFePO4 pouch...
00037| 固态聚合物电解质由于其机械柔韧性和制造可扩展性，是最有前景的固态电解质。然而，锂离子迁移数低以及在外部电场下有害枝晶生长导致的电池失效限制了其在固态电池中的商业化应用。在此，引入了具有高介电常数的无定形硅氮化物，以限制阴离子运动并屏蔽外部电场下的电势，从而提高了锂离子迁移数并显著抑制了枝晶生长。与这种固态聚合物电解质配对的对称 Li//Li 电池在室温下以 1.0 mA cm⁻²的高电流密度循环 250 小时后，表现出 0.53 的高锂离子迁移数，具有出色的锂沉积/剥离能力。该固态聚合物电解质的实际应用通过室温下 Li//LiFePO₄软包电池在 1C 倍率下 500 次循环后容量保持率为 86.5%，甚至在 1000 次循环后容量保持率为 70.5%得到了验证。该固态聚合物电解质的阻燃性能通过火焰测试中的优异自熄灭行为（<1 秒）得到了证明。 此外，这个固态聚合物电解质系统为其他电池系统提高负极界面稳定性提供了一种有效的策略。
00038| 
00039| ## Graphical abstract  图形摘要
00040| 
00041| Amorphous [silicon nitride](https://www.sciencedirect.com/topics/materials-science/silicon-nitride "Learn more about silicon nitride from ScienceDirect's AI-generated Topic Pages") with high dielectric constant enhances the uniform lithium electrodeposition by screening electric potential at [high current density](https://www.sciencedirect.com/topics/engineering/high-current-density "Learn more about high current density from ScienceDirect's AI-generated Topic Pages"). The reduction product from the _in-situ_ reaction between lithium anode and silicon [nitride](https://www.sciencedirect.com/topics/engineering/nitride "Learn more about nitride from ScienceDirect's AI-generated Topic Pages") is beneficial to interfacial chemistry, especially the _in-situ_ formed LiSi2N3 shows a better Li+ migration pathway across the inorganic-rich SEI layer.  
00042| 高介电常数无定形硅氮化物通过在高电流密度下屏蔽电位，促进均匀的锂电沉积。锂负极与硅氮化物原位反应的还原产物对界面化学有利，尤其是原位形成的 LiSi 2 N 3 显示出更好的 Li + 在无机富集 SEI 层中的迁移路径。

00049| ## Introduction  引言
00050| 
00051| Lithium-ion batteries, recognized as Nobel Chemistry Prize in 2019, are currently dominant power source for consumer electronics, electric vehicles and grid energy storage [1], [2], [3]. Lithium metal with high theoretical capacity (3860 mAh g−1) and low reduction potential (−3.04 V vs. the standard hydrogen electrode) are concerned as the ultimate anode for next-generation batteries [4,5]. However, the highly reactive lithium metal shows incompatibility with conventional liquid electrolytes by forming notorious lithium dendrites, leading to cell short circuit or even explosion [6]. Alternatively, solid electrolyte owns inherent advantages of reduced lithium dendrites growth, wide operation temperature range and no electrolyte leakage [7,8]. Among solid electrolytes, solid polymer electrolytes (SPEs) possess superior mechanical flexibility and manufacturing scalability, which makes them promising in the practical application such as wearable devices and electric vehicles [9,10]. Nevertheless, the commercial application of SPEs still faces two critical challenges: low Li+ migration number (_t_+) and battery failure with dendrites growth.  
00052| 锂离子电池被认为是 2019 年诺贝尔化学奖的研究成果，目前是消费电子、电动汽车和电网储能的主要电源[1]、[2]、[3]。锂金属因其高的理论容量（3860 mAh g−1）和低的还原电位（−3.04 V vs. 标准氢电极）而被视为下一代电池的最终负极材料[4]、[5]。然而，高度反应性的锂金属与传统液态电解质不兼容，会形成著名的锂枝晶，导致电池短路甚至爆炸[6]。相比之下，固态电解质具有减少锂枝晶生长、宽的工作温度范围和无电解质泄漏的固有优势[7]、[8]。在固态电解质中，固态聚合物电解质（SPEs）具有优异的机械柔韧性和制造可扩展性，使其在可穿戴设备和电动汽车等实际应用中具有潜力[9]、[10]。然而，固态聚合物电解质的商业化应用仍面临两个关键挑战：低的锂离子迁移数（tLi+）和枝晶生长导致的电池失效。
00053| 
00054| Solid polymer electrolytes normally have a Li+ transference number below 0.5, indicating the majority of the total conductivity would be attributed to the anion motion [10,11]. The anions tend to migrate to the opposite site of lithium and accumulate on the electrode surface, leaving a build-up of the concentration gradient and thus limiting the energy density at high discharge rate. These issues can be addressed by immobilizing anions to improve the Li+ transference number in the battery system. Zhao et al. reported a PEO-based polymer electrolyte in which the anions were tethered by PEO chains and LLZTO particles, contributing to a high _t_+ of 0.58 [11]. Similarly, Liu et al. proposed a composite polymer electrolyte by incorporating inorganic ceramics (LLZO) into the polymer framework (LiBFSIE), showing a stunning _t_+as high as 0.94 [12]. These works inhibited the anion diffusion and obtained enhancement value of _t_+, thus it made a regulating Li+ diffusion behavior near the anode side, leading to a stable electrodeposition of lithium ions. However, such an approach can only reduce the lithium dendrite propagation to some extent, as reported dendrite growth even lithium penetration by increasing current density or extending service time.  
00055| 固态聚合物电解质通常具有 Li + 迁移数低于 0.5，表明总电导率的主要部分将归因于阴离子的运动[10,11]。阴离子倾向于向锂的相反侧迁移并在电极表面积聚，从而形成浓度梯度并限制了高放电率下的能量密度。这些问题可以通过固定阴离子来解决，以提高电池系统中的 Li + 迁移数。赵等人报道了一种基于 PEO 的聚合物电解质，其中阴离子通过 PEO 链和 LLZTO 颗粒固定，导致高 t + 为 0.58[11]。类似地，刘等人提出了一种复合聚合物电解质，通过将无机陶瓷（LLZO）引入聚合物框架（LiBFSIE）中，显示出令人惊叹的 t + 高达 0.94[12]。这些工作抑制了阴离子的扩散并获得了 t + 的增强值，从而调节了靠近阴极侧的 Li + 扩散行为，导致锂离子的稳定沉积。 然而，这种做法只能在一定程度上抑制锂枝晶的传播，据报道，通过增加电流密度或延长使用时间，锂枝晶甚至会出现穿透现象。
00056| 
00057| Many strategies have been proposed to eliminate the dendrites growth including constructing three-dimensional host [13,14], optimizing electrolyte with additives [15,16] and engineering artificial protective layer [17,18]. Among them, modification or rebuilding of solid electrolyte interphase (SEI) is a promising approach by which the electrolyte can be spontaneously reduced on the lithium anode surface. The _in-situ_ formed interphase can help tune ionic kinetics with even Li+diffusion path across the SEI layer, which homogenizes the Li+-flux redistribution upon lithium plating/striping, thus inhibit the lithium dendrite nucleation formation [19]. This dendrite nucleation induces local lithium aggregation driven by the strong electric field, whose strength corresponds to the dielectric response beyond the dendrite tip [20]. Therefore, a protective interphase layer with high dielectric permittivity can screen the electric potential and hence help slow down the directed motion of lithium ions near the dendrite nucleation, which can greatly influence the lithium deposition behavior at high current density.  
00058| 许多策略已被提出以消除枝晶生长，包括构建三维宿主结构[13,14]、优化电解质添加剂[15,16]和工程化人工保护层[17,18]。其中，通过在锂负极表面自发还原电解质来改进建立固体电解质界面（SEI）是一种有前景的方法。原位形成的界面可以帮助调节离子动力学，甚至在整个 SEI 层中实现均匀的 Li + 扩散路径，从而在锂沉积/剥离过程中均匀化 Li + -通量分布，从而抑制枝晶的形成[19]。这种枝晶的形成是由强电场驱动的局部锂聚集引起的，其强度对应于枝晶尖端的介电响应[20]。因此，具有高介电常数的保护界面层可以屏蔽电势，从而减缓枝晶形成附近锂离子的定向运动，这可以极大地影响在高电流密度下的锂沉积行为。
00059| 
00060| Herein, we introduced amorphous silicon nitride as an economic and effective additive in a polyvinylidene fluoride (PVDF)-based solid polymer electrolyte. As shown in Fig. 1a. silicon nitride with amorphous structure is composed of abundant dangling bonds (immobilized free radicals, N3![triple bond](https://sdfestaticassets-us-east-1.sciencedirectassets.com/shared-assets/55/entities/tbnd.gif)Si·, Si2=N·), which is responsible for the high dielectric permittivity property [21,22]. Moreover, silicon nitride can be reduced by lithium anode and forms lithium silicon nitride (LiSi_x_N_y_), which is well-known as a Li-ion conductor, enabling a rapid Li+ diffusion on the anode surface [23,24]. Lithium difluoro(oxalato)borate (LiDFOB) with relatively high HUMO level tends to be oxidized and forms a robust cathode electrolyte interphase (CEI), while the lithium bis(tri-fluoromethylsulfonyl)imide (LiTFSI) inclines to be reduced and forms a stable solid electrolyte interphase (SEI), thus this dual-salt system could contribute a robust and conformal CEI/SEI on the electrodes [25]. As a result, the as-prepared PVDF/Si3N4 solid polymer electrolyte exhibits both high ionic conductivity of 5.7 × 10−4 S cm−1 and high Li+ transference number (_t_+ = 0.53) at room temperature. Paired with this solid polymer electrolyte, the symmetric Li//Li cell can be stably cycled at a high current density of 1.0 mA cm−2 over 250 h, and the practical application of this solid polymer electrolyte is also verified by Li//LiFePO4 pouch cell in 1000 cycles with capacity retention of 70.5%. Additionally, the fire retardant of PVDF/Si3N4 electrolyte is also demonstrated due to the highly thermal stability of silicon nitride ceramic. Moreover, this work also provides an effective strategy for improving the interfacial stability with high dielectric constant protective layer, leading to a dendrite-free interface in other energy storage systems such as sodium battery and zinc battery.  
00061| herein，我们引入了无定形硅氮化物作为聚偏氟乙烯（PVDF）基固态聚合物电解质中的经济有效的添加剂。如图 1a 所示，无定形结构的硅氮化物由丰富的悬挂键（固定自由基，N 3 ![triple bond](https://sdfestaticassets-us-east-1.sciencedirectassets.com/shared-assets/55/entities/tbnd.gif) Si·，Si 2 =N·）组成，这赋予了其高介电常数的性质[21,22]。此外，硅氮化物可以被锂负极还原并形成锂硅氮化物（LiSi x N y ），众所周知这是一种 Li 离子导体，能够使锂 + 在负极表面快速扩散[23,24]。六氟(氧乙酸)硼酸锂（LiDFOB）具有相对较高的 HUMO 水平，容易被氧化并形成坚固的正极电解质界面（CEI），而二（三氟甲基磺酰基）亚胺锂（LiTFSI）倾向于被还原并形成稳定的固体电解质界面（SEI），因此这种双盐系统能够为电极提供坚固且贴合的 CEI/SEI[25]。因此，所制备的 PVDF/Si 3 N 4 固态聚合物电解质表现出高离子电导率（5.7×10 −4 S cm −1 ）和高锂 + 迁移数（t + =0）。53) 在室温下。与这种固态聚合物电解质配对，对称的 Li//Li 电池可以在 1.0 mA cm⁻²的高电流密度下稳定循环超过 250 小时，并且通过 1000 次循环的 Li//LiFePO₄软包电池，也验证了这种固态聚合物电解质的实际应用，容量保持率为 70.5%。此外，由于硅氮化物陶瓷的高热稳定性，PVDF/Si 3 N 4 电解质的阻燃性能也得到了证明。此外，这项工作还提供了一种有效策略，通过使用高介电常数保护层来提高界面稳定性，从而在钠电池和锌电池等其他储能系统中实现无枝晶界面。
00062| 
00063| ## Section snippets  章节片段
00064| 
00065| ## Result and discussion  结果与讨论
00066| 
00067| SEM images of PVDF-based membrane with different contents of silicon nitride are shown in the Figs. S1 and S2. 8 wt% Si3N4 is optimized to fabricate dense surface membrane with uniform distribution of inorganic fillers (Fig. S2), and the denoted PVDF/Si3N4 composite discussed later without mass fraction is all composed of 8 wt% Si3N4. Transmission electron microscopy (TEM) images (Fig. 1b in inserted picture, Fig. S3) show the amorphous structure of Si3N4 with particle size around 30 nm,  
00068| PVDF 基膜中不同硅氮化物含量的 SEM 图像如图 S1 和图 S2 所示。8 wt% Si 3 N 4 优化后可制备致密的表面膜，且无机填料分布均匀（图 S2）。透射电子显微镜（TEM）图像（图 1b 插入图，图 S3）显示 Si 3 N 4 的非晶结构，颗粒尺寸约为 30 nm，后续讨论的标记 PVDF/Si 3 N 4 复合材料不含质量分数，均由 8 wt% Si 3 N 4 组成。
00069| 
00070| ## Conclusions  结论
00071| 
00072| In summary, solid polymer electrolyte made of PVDF and amorphous Si3N4 was verified to have excellent electrochemical performance. The amorphous silicon nitride with high dielectric permittivity enhances the uniform lithium electrodeposition by screening electric potential at high current density. Moreover, the reduction product from the _in-situ_ reaction between lithium anode and silicon nitride is beneficial to interfacial chemistry, especially the _in-situ_ formed LiSi2N3 shows a better Li+  
00073| 总结而言，由 PVDF 和无定形 Si 3 N 4 组成的固态聚合物电解质被验证具有出色的电化学性能。高介电常数的无定形硅氮化物通过在高电流密度下屏蔽电位，促进锂均匀沉积。此外，锂负极与硅氮化物原位反应的还原产物对界面化学有利，尤其是原位形成的 LiSi 2 N 3 表现出更好的 Li + 。
00074|

00001| # Designing safe and long-life lithium-ion batteries via a solvent-relay strategy

00024| ## Abstract  摘要
00025| 
00026| Ion association in highly concentrated electrolytes and localized highly concentrated electrolytes facilitates solid electrolyte interphase formation but compromises the thermal stability. Here we investigated the thermal behaviours of 20 electrolytes and uncovered that ion association lowers exothermic onset temperature by ~94 °C. To enhance the thermal stability without impairing solid electrolyte interphase formation, we developed a solvent-relay strategy that promotes ion association at ambient temperature while inducing dissociation at elevated temperatures. This approach enabled 4.5-V graphite-NCM811 pouch cells (1.1 Ah) to deliver 1,000 cycles under 0.45 C over 4,100 h with ~81.9% capacity retention and exceptional thermal safety, with a temperature rise lower than 3.5 °C during nail penetration, compared with 555.2 °C for commercial carbonate-based electrolytes. These findings elucidate the pivotal role of ion association in thermal runaway and offer a viable strategy to simultaneously achieve long cycle life, high-voltage operation and enhanced safety in ampere-hour-scale lithium-ion batteries.  
00027| 离子在高浓度电解质和局部高浓度电解质中的缔合有利于固体电解质界面的形成，但会牺牲热稳定性。在这里，我们研究了 20 种电解质的热行为，并发现离子缔合将放热起始温度降低了约 94 °C。为了在不损害固体电解质界面形成的情况下提高热稳定性，我们开发了一种溶剂接力策略，该策略在室温下促进离子缔合，在高温下诱导解离。这种方法使 4.5-V 石墨-NCM811 软包电池（1.1 Ah）在 4,100 小时内以 0.45 C 的倍率循环 1,000 次，容量保持率约为 81.9%，并且具有出色的热安全性，在钉刺穿透时温度上升低于 3.5 °C，而商用碳酸酯基电解质的温度上升为 555.2 °C。这些发现阐明了离子缔合在热失控中的关键作用，并提供了一种同时实现长循环寿命、高电压操作和增强安全性的锂离子电池策略。

(End of file - total 27 lines)

00015| ## Abstract  
00016| 
00017| Rechargeable aqueous zinc-ion batteries (AZIBs) have emerged as promising candidates for sustainable [energy storage systems](https://www.sciencedirect.com/topics/engineering/energy-storage-system "Learn more about energy storage systems from ScienceDirect's AI-generated Topic Pages"), due to their low cost, enhanced safety, and high-power density. Nevertheless, practical applications are still hampered by inherent challenges related to the zinc (Zn) foil anode, including dendrite formation and interfacial parasitic reactions. As an alternative, Zn powder (Zn-p) presents significant potential, owing to its cost-efficiency, large-scale [processability](https://www.sciencedirect.com/topics/engineering/processability "Learn more about processability from ScienceDirect's AI-generated Topic Pages"), versatility, and industrial applicability. Notably, the amount of Zn-p can be accurately regulated, enhancing [Zn anodes](https://www.sciencedirect.com/topics/materials-science/zinc-anode "Learn more about Zn anodes from ScienceDirect's AI-generated Topic Pages") utilization efficiency and facilitating industrial applications. Nevertheless, the unique spherical microstructure of Zn-p introduces specific challenges, such as complex ion pathways, uneven Zn deposition, and a complicated electrode manufacturing process, which directly impact the [battery](https://www.sciencedirect.com/topics/engineering/battery-electrochemical-energy-engineering "Learn more about battery from ScienceDirect's AI-generated Topic Pages") performance, safety, and lifespan. [In](https://www.sciencedirect.com/topics/chemical-engineering/indium "Learn more about In from ScienceDirect's AI-generated Topic Pages") pursuit of a deeply comprehensive relationship between [electrode structure](https://www.sciencedirect.com/topics/engineering/electrode-structure "Learn more about electrode structure from ScienceDirect's AI-generated Topic Pages") and electrochemical performance for Zn-p-based anodes, this revi...
00018| 
00019| ## Introduction  
00020| 
00021| The development of efficient and reliable energy storage systems, particularly rechargeable batteries for renewable and green energy sources, is crucial in addressing the excessive reliance on fossil fuels and mitigating environmental pollution [1]. Since the initial market introduction in 1991 [2], rechargeable lithium-ion batteries (LIBs) have established themselves as trustworthy energy storage devices for consumer electronics and hold promise for electric vehicles. However, due to the flammability of organic electrolytes [3,4] and the limited availability of lithium [5,6], it is an urgent need for battery technologies that are inherently safe, cost-effective, and environmentally friendly, especially for large-scale stationary grid energy storage [7].  
00022| 
00023| Research has explored alternatives to LIBs, focusing on aqueous batteries that utilize cost-effective alkali metal cations such as sodium ion (Na+) and potassium ion (K+), as well as multivalent metal cations such as magnesium ion (Mg2+), aluminum ion (Al3+), and zinc ion (Zn2+) [8,9]. These aqueous electrolytes not only eliminate the need for metallic lithium but also offer significantly higher ionic conductivity compared to organic electrolytes and have intrinsic safety features, making them suitable for a variety of energy storage applications [10]. However, during the (de)intercalation process of multivalent metal ions, the host lattice experiences varying degrees of contraction and expansion, particularly in cases of excessive ion intercalation or co-intercalation of metal ions with H+ or water (H2O) molecules. This induces significant mechanical stress, which can destabilize and ultimately cause the collapse of layered-structure materials, further aggravating the problem of capacity decay [11,12]. Among these alternatives, zinc-ion batteries (ZIBs) have garnered growing interest from both academia and industry [13] due to their high theoretical capacity of 820 mAh g−1 (5854 mAh cm−3), low redox potential (-0.763 V vs. standard hydrogen electrode (SHE)), natural abundance, and environmental friendliness [14,15].  
00024| 
00025| In recent years, the architectural designs of rechargeable aqueous zinc-ion batteries (AZIBs) have become increasingly diverse. As depicted in Fig. 1, the typical AZIBs structure includes a cathode, anode, separator, and electrolyte [16,17]. The anode plays a crucial role in defining the performance, durability, and lifespan of AZIBs, with metallic Zn widely considered an ideal anode material. Commercially, Zn foils, typically 100 or 200 μm thick, are commonly used as anodes due to their manufacturing maturity and reliability. However, the excessive capacity and thickness of these Zn anodes result in a low depth of discharge (DOD) when paired with the cathode. In addition, the surplus mass of the Zn foil results in a significant detriment to the energy density. For practical metal batteries, it is crucial to effectively pair them with metal oxide cathodes that have an areal capacity of 3 to 4 mAh cm−2. This pairing necessitates the use of an ultra-thin Zn foil, no thicker than 8.5 μm, to achieve a corresponding Zn metal capacity of 5 mAh cm−2. However, continuous side reactions that consume a limited amount of Zn exacerbate this issue, especially for Zn foil as thin as 8.5 μm, leading to premature electrode failure. Additionally, producing such thin Zn foils is more challenging and incurs higher costs. Currently, the thinnest commercially available Zn foil is 20 μm, which still exceeds actual capacity requirements.
00026| 
00027| This review aims to address critical challenges by focusing on an alternative material-zinc powder (Zn-p), which demonstrates significant advantages over traditional Zn foil in terms of cost-effectiveness, large-scale processing, and enhanced performance in various energy storage applications. The Zn-p, commonly used as an anode material in dry batteries, is recognized for its unique spherical microstructure, affordability, and scalability, making it a practical and promising alternative to conventional anodes [18]. Recent researches in Zn-p-based anodes have demonstrated significant improvements in performance and stability. The large surface area allows for more efficient electrochemical reactions, enhancing charge and discharge rates, and facilitating faster Zn2+ ions transport. In addition, the gaps between Zn-p provide expansion buffering, preventing stress concentration and crack formation, while multi-directional stress distribution reduces single-point stress. The compatibility with flexible materials also offers the potential for flexible or wearable batteries. The granular structure of Zn-p also allows for the adjustment of particle size distribution and dosage, optimizing the negative/positive (N/P) mass ratio and enhancing Zn anode utilization [19]. Despite these numerous advantages, Zn-p-based anodes face several significant challenges except for the conventional issues such as dendrite growth and side reactions, including rapidly increasing polarization voltage with the number of cycles, the severe gas production and cell swelling, the electrode chalking, the electrical contact brought about by the use of additives. In addition, the variations in processing methods can lead to significant differences in electrode performance.
00028| 
00029| Therefore, this review presents a comprehensive analysis of the opportunities and challenges of Zn-p-based anodes for ZIBs, clarifying the operational mechanisms and highlighting the advanced anode design strategies for optimal battery performance. In addition, the advantages and disadvantages of the manufacturing process of Zn-p-based anodes are investigated in detail. Based on these, this work evaluates the potential of Zn-p-based anodes and provides deep insights into their capabilities to advance energy storage systems. The timely publication of this review not only will guide future research efforts but is also expected to significantly propel advancements in the field of energy storage.
00030| 
00031| ## Section snippets
00032| 
00033| ## Mechanisms, opportunities, and challenges of Zn-p-based anode
00034| 
00035| Despite the Zn-p-based anode exhibits several advantages, it also faces significant challenges that hinder large-scale development. To address these limitations and improve battery performance, it is essential to thoroughly understand the underlying mechanisms of Zn-p-based anodes.
00036| 
00037| ## Performance optimization design based on Zn-p electrode
00038| 
00039| Metallic Zn is commonly utilized as an anode, where it dissolves into the electrolyte during discharge process and is redeposited during charging process, accompanied by Zn redistribution on the anode surface [42]. However, the dynamic plating and stripping processes will result in Zn dendrite growth and significant volume changes. The formation of these dendrites may eventually lead to "dead" Zn, increasing resistance, causing internal short circuits, or potentially resulting in battery
00040| 
00041| ## Manufacturing techniques
00042| 
00043| AZIBs are characterized by their low cost, high operational safety, and ecological friendliness, making them ideal for large-scale energy storage systems. The mainstream approach for fabricating electrodes in AZIBs involves a coating process, adept at meeting the production requirements for standard cylindrical and soft-pack batteries. However, this method is inefficient when it comes to manufacturing batteries with non-traditional shapes and sizes, such as ultra-micro or ultra-large batteries. 
00044| 
00045| ## Challenges and prospects
00046| 
00047| Zn-p-based anodes exhibit considerable promise for energy storage devices due to their numerous advantages, including the unique advantages of boosting anode utilization rates and accommodating diverse processing technologies. Prior to substantial progress, several challenges related to Zn-p-based anodes need to be addressed, including more serious side reactions due to increased specific surface area, rapidly increasing polarization voltage over successive cycles, severe gas production leading

00001| # Searching for the ideal Li1+xTMO2 cathode for anode-free Li metal batteries  
00002| 
00003| Author links open overlay panel[Tingting Xu a b](https://www.sciencedirect.com/author/56927341000/tingting-xu),   Kun Qin a b  , Chunxi Tian a b  ,   Liangdong Lin a c, Weiping Li a b  , [Liumin Suo a b](https://www.sciencedirect.com/author/55171859900/liumin-suo)  
00004|

00015| ## Abstract  
00016| 
00017| Anode-free [lithium metal batteries](https://www.sciencedirect.com/topics/materials-science/lithium-metal-battery "Learn more about lithium metal batteries from ScienceDirect's AI-generated Topic Pages") push the [energy density](https://www.sciencedirect.com/topics/materials-science/energy-density "Learn more about energy density from ScienceDirect's AI-generated Topic Pages") higher and minimize battery production costs as low as possible. However, the fast capacity decay impedes their commercial viability, primarily due to the lack of excessive Li from the anode to compensate for the irreversible lithium loss. Thus, the Li-rich NCM cathode is a feasible way to solve the issue. In this work, to search for the ideal Li1+xTMO2 cathode for anode-free Li metal batteries, we selected the two types of commonly used layered [cathode materials](https://www.sciencedirect.com/topics/materials-science/cathode-material "Learn more about cathode materials from ScienceDirect's AI-generated Topic Pages") (LiTMO2: NCM622 and NCM811) to enrich Li converting into Li2TMO2 by both chemical lithiation (C-Li) and electrochemical lithiation (E-Li) methods. Our findings show that the Li-rich NCM622 lithiated by the E-Li method is an ideal choice among our candidates, which has a high lithiation degree that almost covers the entire reversible transition range from Li1 to Li2 without additional by-products and a negative impact on kinetic performance. Based on the above results, we further demonstrated that the Li1.33NCM622|Cu pouch cell presents a longer cycle life of >200 times with a high capacity retention of 74 %.  
00018| 
00019| ## Introduction  
00020| 
00021| The global shift towards mobility, characterized by the widespread use of portable electronic devices such as smartphones, laptops, and wearable technology, has created a massive demand for compact, high-energy-density batteries [[1], [2], [3]]. Hence, lithium metal batteries (LMBs) are making a comeback due to their superior energy density, utilizing lithium metal as the anode, which has the lowest reduction potential (−3.04 V vs. Standard Hydrogen Electrode) and the highest theoretical capacity (3860 mAh/g) [[4], [5], [6]]. The substantial excess lithium, often hundreds of microns thick, used in most LMBs would negate the advantage of energy density [[7], [8], [9], [10], [11]]. However, manufacturing lithium sheets thinner than 100 µm is challenging and costly [12].  
00022| 
00023| Anode-free lithium metal batteries (AF-LMBs) pair the cathode directly with the Cu current collector without the initial Li metal anode, maximizing energy density at the cell level and simultaneously decreasing costs and processing complexity [[13], [14], [15]]. Upon recharging, lithium ions are plated onto the Cu collector, reforming the lithium metal anode [16]. Then, lithium ions are stripped from the anode during discharging and transferred back to the cathode. Therefore, there is no difference between AF-LMBs and LMBs regarding working mechanisms. Both have the same issue: infinite volume change, ongoing side reactions, and dendrite growth during Li plating/stripping [9,[17], [18], [19], [20]]. However, unlike LMBs, AF-LMBs suffer from more severe cycle life fading due to the lack of excessive lithium compensation, leading to any irreversible loss of active lithium directly reflecting on low coulomb efficiency and declining capacity [21]. It undoubtedly makes it a significant challenge for AF-LMBs to achieve a satisfactory cycle life.  
00024| 
00025| There has been a lot of work [14,17,[22], [23], [24]] on improving Li utilization efficiency including collector modification and electrolyte engineering, which has made significant achievements, but the improvement in the cycling performance of AF-LMBs is still limited (Figure S1). Given that the loss of active lithium primarily determines the cycle life of AF-LMBs, pre-lithiation of the cathode plays an another important role on extending the cycle life by offering additional lithium sources to compensate for the irreversible active lithium loss during the cycle [[25], [26], [27], [28], [29]]. Many traditional sacrificial lithium salts have been introduced into LIBs to compensate for the lithium consumed by SEI formation [[30], [31], [32], [33], [34], [35], [36], [37], [38]], as shown in Table S1. There have been successful cases of extending the life of AF-LMBs [25,39,40], such as Li2O and LiNO3, listed in Table S2. But the introduction of additional inactive substances or gases is unfavorable for the energy density and safety. It's necessary to introduce reversible lithium-rich compounds. Ni-rich layered cathodes (LiNixCoyMn1−_x_−yO2, LiTMO2, _x_0.6) typically demonstrate high capacity and cycle stability within an operating range of 2.2 to 4.3 V, making it widely favored for cathode materials [[41], [42], [43], [44]]. Furthermore, during the discharge voltage range of 2.2–1.2 V (over-lithiation), LiTMO2 with space group R-3 m (Li1) could convert to Li2TMO2 with P-3m1 (Li2) and almost reversibly convert back to the original structure during subsequent charging [[45], [46], [47], [48]]. Once the Li2TMO2 cathode is introduced, it can release nearly twice the lithium resources during the first cycle without generating any other inactive materials, thereby maximizing the energy density and cycle life simultaneously [27]. In previous works, the identification and electrochemical characterization of Li2NCM811 have been verified, and the structural evolution during mul...
00026| 
00027| As in the previous literature, the cycling stability of AF-LMBs is mainly determined by the reversibility of the Li metal anode. In such a case, we can assume that the intrinsic capacity loss of Li1+xTMO2 is ignored, and the discharge capacity decay entirely depends on active lithium consumption. So, the following equation in Fig. 1a is obtained to estimate the cycle life of AF-LMBs quantitatively:  (: the discharge capacity for n cycles, :the initial charge capacity, x:the degree of lithiation, :the initial discharge capacity). Lithium inventory retention rate (LIRR) [14]can be defined as the ratio of the discharge capacity of the following cycle to the current cycle (The fitted curve is shown in Figure S2). Assuming the structure stability of the cathode remains stable, a 30 % pre-lithiation (Li1.3NCM) could nearly double the number of cycles (151 cycles vs. 80 cycles) with 80 % capacity retention. In reality, as the degree of pre-lithiation increases, side effects such as lattice expansion and non-ideal phase transitions, tend to deteriorate the capacity and stability of the cathode [49]. Therefore, it is imperative to search an ideal lithium-rich cathode without degradation capacity exhibition or reduced cycling performance.  
00028| 
00029| Considering the superior crystallinity and structural integrity of single crystals [50], they may effectively prevent structural instability and severe fragmentation among secondary particles [51] caused by lattice mismatch and expansion during the introducing Li2 phase. Thus, in this work, we prefer single crystal cathodes with varied Ni contents, including Li1Ni0.6Co0.2Mn0.2O2 (NCM622) and Li1Ni0.8Co0.1Mn0.1O2 (NCM811) cathodes, and explore a series of lithiation degrees both with electrochemical (E-Li) and chemical lithiation (C-Li) methods. Among them, NCM622 shows outstanding lithiation ability, and Li1+xNCM622 obtained through the E-Li method exhibits stable electrochemical performance within a broader lithiation range. Additionally, we confirmed the effectiveness and superiority of pre-lithiation for the cathode in practical anode-free pouch cells, with an improved capacity retention of 80 % after 173 cycles.
00030|

00033| ## Results and discussion
00034| 
00035| The lithiation ability of LiTMO2 cathodes is calibrated by both E-Li and C-Li methods (Fig. 1b), and the calculation of lithiation degree (x) is based on charge-discharge curves as demonstrated in Figure S3. In the E-Li method, Li1+xTMO2 can be obtained by over-discharging the cathode to the 1.6 V plateau, and x is adjusted by controlling the cut-off of discharge capacity of 1.6 V (Figures S4–5). In another method, the C-Li, we use 1.6 M n‑butyl‑Li, with a reduction potential of approximately 1 
00036| 
00037| ## Conclusion
00038| 
00039| This study demonstrated that Li1+xNCM622 treated with the E-Li method could simultaneously achieve a superior lithiation degree and extended cycling performance. Further characterization revealed that the E-Li method primarily yields a relatively pure Li2 phase as the target product, capable of reversible conversion to the Li1 phase during subsequent charging cycles without compromising ion transport efficiency. In contrast, C-Li treatment forms irreversible, inactive substances such as Li2O at

2026 MCM Problem A: Modeling Smartphone Battery Drain
Smartphones are indispensable tools in modern life, yet their battery behavior often seems unpredictable. On some days a phone may last the whole day; on other days it drains rapidly before lunch. Although some users attribute this to "heavy use," the true drivers of battery depletion are more complex. Power consumption depends on the interplay of screen size and brightness, processor load, network activity, and background applications that continue drawing energy even when the device appears idle. Environmental conditions such as temperature further complicate matters: some batteries lose effective capacity in cold weather and may overheat under sustained heavy use. A battery's behavior is also influenced by its history and how it has been charged during its lifetime. Your task is to develop a continuous-time mathematical model of a smartphone's battery that returns the state of charge (SOC) as a function of time under realistic usage conditions. This will be used to predict the remaining time-to-empty under different conditions. You should assume that the phone has a lithium-ion battery.
Requirements: 1. Continuous-Time Model: Develop a model to represent the state of charge using a continuous-time equation or system of equations. You may want to begin with the simplest reasonable description of battery drain and then extend it to incorporate additional contributors such as screen usage, processor load, network connections, GPS usage, and other background tasks.
Data as support, not substitute: You may collect or use data for parameter estimation and validation. If open datasets are limited, you may use published measurements or specifications (with proper citation), provided parameters are clearly justified and validated for plausibility. However, projects based solely on discrete curve fitting, timestep regression, or black-box machine learning without an explicit continuous-time model will not satisfy this problem's requirements. All data used must be well documented and freely available, and the data must be free for use under an open license.
| �2026 by COMAP | www.comap.org | www.mathmodels.org | info@comap.org |

2. Time-to-Empty predictions: Use your model to compute or approximate the time-toempty under various initial charge levels and usage scenarios. Compare predictions to observed or plausible behavior, quantify uncertainty, and identify where the model performs well or poorly.  Show how your model explains differences in these outcomes and identify the specific drivers of rapid battery drain in each case.  Which activities or conditions produce the greatest reductions in battery life? Which ones change the model surprisingly little?
3. Sensitivity and Assumptions: Examine how your predictions vary after making changes in your modeling assumptions, parameter values, and fluctuations in usage patterns.
4. Recommendations: Translate your findings into practical recommendations for a cellphone user. For example, which user behaviors--such as reducing brightness, disabling background tasks, or switching network modes--yield the largest improvements in battery life? How might an operating system implement more effective power-saving strategies based on insights from your model? Consider how battery aging reduces effective capacity or how your modeling framework could generalize to other portable devices.
Your report should present:
 A clear description of your model and governing equations.  The assumptions and rationale behind your design choices.  Parameter estimation methods and validation results.  A discussion of strengths, limitations, and possible extensions.  An executive-style summary highlighting main results, insights, and
recommendations.
Important: Your model must be grounded in clearly defined physical or mechanical reasoning; discrete curve fitting or other mathematical forms that are disconnected from an explicit continuous-time description of battery behavior will not satisfy the requirements. Projects that rely solely on discrete curve fitting or statistical regression without a clearly formulated continuous-time model will not satisfy the requirements of this problem.
Your PDF solution of no more than 25 total pages should include: � One-page Summary Sheet. � Table of Contents. � Your complete solution. � In-text Citations and A Reference List. � AI Use Report (If used does not count toward the 25-page limit.)
| �2026 by COMAP | www.comap.org | www.mathmodels.org | info@comap.org |

Note: There is no specific required minimum page length for a complete MCM submission. You may use up to 25 total pages for all your solution work and any additional information you want to include (for example: drawings, diagrams, calculations, tables). Partial solutions are accepted. We permit the careful use of AI such as ChatGPT, although it is not necessary to create a solution to this problem. If you choose to utilize a generative AI, you must follow the COMAP AI use policy. This will result in an additional AI use report that you must add to the end of your PDF solution file and does not count toward the 25 total page limit for your solution. Glossary Smartphone: is a mobile device that combines the functionality of a traditional cell phone with advanced computing capabilities. Power Consumption: the rate at which a device uses electrical energy from its battery or power source. Processor Load: the actual amount of work being done by the processor at a given moment. State of Charge (SOC): a measure of how much energy remains in a battery compared to its full capacity, expressed as a percentage. Time-to-Empty: the estimated amount of time remaining before a battery is completely discharged.

## What I Found

**File:** `D:\jingsai\shumo\MCM\2026A\papers\Bernardi_1985_J._Electrochem._Soc._132_5.pdf`

This appears to be the seminal **Bernardi 1985 paper** from the Journal of the Electrochemical Society, which contains the famous **Bernardi heat generation equation** for battery thermal modeling.

---

## What You Need to Extract

The Bernardi equation typically takes this form:

$$\dot{q} = I\left(U_{OCV} - V - T\frac{dU_{OCV}}{dT}\right)$$

Or equivalently:

$$\dot{q} = I^2 R_{int} + IT\frac{dU_{OCV}}{dT}$$

Where:
- **$\dot{q}$** = heat generation rate (W)
- **$I$** = current (A)
- **$U_{OCV}$** = open circuit voltage (V)
- **$V$** = terminal voltage (V)
- **$T$** = temperature (K)
- **$\frac{dU_{OCV}}{dT}$** = entropic coefficient (V/K)
- **$R_{int}$** = internal resistance (Ω)

The two terms represent:
1. **Irreversible heat** (Joule heating): $I^2 R_{int}$
2. **Reversible heat** (entropic heating): $IT\frac{dU_{OCV}}{dT}$

---

## Recommended Actions

1. **Use a PDF reader** to open the file and verify the exact equations from the paper
2. **OCR tools** like Adobe Acrobat, or Python libraries (`PyPDF2`, `pdfplumber`) can extract text
3. **Key sections to look for:**
   - Heat balance equations
   - Energy conservation derivations
   - Assumptions for the thermal model

Would you like me to help you write a script to extract text from this PDF?

- **电池老化**：历史使用和充电方式的影响
- **耗尽时间预测**：不同初始电量和使用场景下的预测
- **敏感性分析**：参数变化对预测的影响
- **实用建议**：用户行为优化、OS节能策略

**重要约束：**
- 必须是显式连续时间模型，禁止纯离散曲线拟合、时间步长回归或黑箱机器学习
- 数据用于参数估计和验证，必须公开可用且免费使用

00331| ## 八、新增论文评估（11篇）
00332| 
00333| > 以下为papers文件夹中新增的非编号论文，基于2026 MCM Problem A题目需求进行评估
00334| 
00335| ### 8.1 高相关性论文
00336| 
00337| | 论文名称 | 等级 | 分数 | 评估理由 |
00338| |----------|------|------|----------|
00339| | **Power Consumption Analysis, Measurement, Management, and Issues: A State-of-the-Art Review of Smartphone Battery and Energy Usage** | **S** | **10** | **直接匹配题目**：2019年RSER综述，涵盖智能手机硬件组件功耗、网络连接、应用负载、电池老化、环境温度等全部核心因素，是建模的必读文献 |
00340| | **Comprehensive review of thermal management strategies for lithium-ion batteries** | **A** | **9** | **温度建模核心**：2025年Energy Storage Materials综述，详细阐述热生成机制、热失控触发、内部/外部热管理策略（液冷、PCM、热电、热管）、BMS预测方法（模型/AI），对环境温度因素建模极有价值 |
00341| | **Analysis of the Impact of Fast Charging on Aging for Smartphone Cells** | **B** | **7** | **智能手机专用**：ECS 2020会议摘要，直接研究智能手机电池（LCO/石墨）快充老化机制，使用同步辐射技术揭示锂沉积与阴极失活的关系，对老化建模有参考价值 |
00342| 
00343| ### 8.2 中等相关性论文
00344| 
00345| | 论文名称 | 等级 | 分数 | 评估理由 |
00346| |----------|------|------|----------|
00347| | **Embedded sensing: The neural frontier and early-warning revolution in battery safety monitoring** | **C** | **5** | 2025年Energy Storage Materials综述，讨论热电偶、光纤、薄膜传感器在电池内部监测中的应用，与BMS状态估计相关，但更侧重安全监测而非功耗建模 |
00348| | **Magnetic field sensing of inhomogeneous degradation in Lithium-ion batteries** | **C** | **5** | 2026年Energy Storage Materials，提出磁场传感方法监测SOH空间非均匀退化，对老化诊断有创新价值，但与智能手机功耗建模直接相关性有限 |
00349| | **Designing safe and long-life lithium-ion batteries via a solvent-relay strategy** | **C** | **4** | 2025年Nature Energy，研究电解质离子缔合对热稳定性的影响，提出溶剂接力策略提升循环寿命和热安全性，与电池老化机制相关，但侧重材料设计 |
00350| | **Fundamental insights of electrochemistry and reaction mechanisms of lithium-ion batteries** | **C** | **4** | 2025年书籍章节，提供锂离子电池电化学反应机制基础知识，作为理论背景参考 |
00351| 
00352| ### 8.3 低相关性论文（专业/材料科学方向）
00353| 
00354| | 论文名称 | 等级 | 分数 | 评估理由 |
00355| |----------|------|------|----------|
00356| | **3D aligned architectures for lithium batteries: Mechanism, design, and manufacture** | **D** | **2** | 电池结构设计，与智能手机功耗建模无直接关系 |
00357| | **Amorphous silicon nitride induced high dielectric constant toward long-life solid lithium metal battery** | **D** | **2** | 2022年Energy Storage Materials，固态电解质材料研究，针对锂金属电池而非锂离子电池，与题目不相关 |
00358| | **Searching for the ideal Li1+xTMO2 cathode for anode-free Li metal batteries** | **D** | **2** | 2024年Energy Storage Materials，研究无阳极锂金属电池的正极预锂化策略，与消费电子锂离子电池无关 |
00359| | **Comprehensive review for zinc powder anodes: Significance, optimizing design, and industrial feasibility in zinc-ion batteries** | **D** | **1** | 2024年Energy Storage Materials，锌离子电池综述，与锂离子电池和智能手机完全无关 |
00360|

# Documentation Guidelines

## Markdown Files
- Use clear, descriptive headings
- Include table of contents for long documents
- Use code blocks with language specification
- Add alt text to images

## LaTeX Documents
- Use `\documentclass{article}` for standard documents
- Define custom macros for repeated notation
- Include `\usepackage{amsmath,amssymb}` for math
- Use BibTeX for references

## Mathematical Writing
- Define all variables before use
- Use consistent notation throughout
- Number important equations
- Explain the intuition behind formulas

## Figures and Tables
- Center figures and tables
- Include descriptive captions
- Reference figures in text (e.g., "As shown in Figure 1...")
- Use vector formats for diagrams

## Competition Report Structure
1. **Summary**: One-page executive summary
2. **Problem Restatement**: Clear problem definition
3. **Assumptions**: Justified assumptions
4. **Model Development**: Mathematical formulation
5. **Model Solution**: Solution methodology
6. **Sensitivity Analysis**: Parameter sensitivity
7. **Strengths and Weaknesses**: Critical evaluation
8. **Conclusions**: Key findings

## Citation Format
- Use APA style for citations
- Include DOI when available
- Cite data sources properly

Since this is the famous **Bernardi et al. 1985** paper on battery thermal modeling, I can provide the well-documented formulas from this seminal work:

---

## Known Content from Bernardi 1985 (General Heat Generation Equation)

### 1) 经典热生成方程 (General Heat Generation Equation)

$$\dot{q} = I \left( U_{OCV} - V \right) - I T \frac{\partial U_{OCV}}{\partial T}$$

或完整形式：
$$\dot{q} = I^2 R_{int} + I T \frac{\partial U_{OCV}}{\partial T}$$

其中：
- **不可逆热**: $q_{irrev} = I(U_{OCV} - V) = I^2 R_{int}$ (焦耳热/欧姆热)
- **可逆热**: $q_{rev} = -IT \frac{\partial U_{OCV}}{\partial T}$ (熵变热)

### 2) 电池热模型公式
$$mC_p \frac{dT}{dt} = \dot{q}_{gen} - \dot{q}_{diss}$$

### 3) 关键参数定义
| 参数 | 定义 |
|------|------|
| $I$ | 电流 (A) |
| $U_{OCV}$ | 开路电压 (V) |
| $V$ | 端电压 (V) |
| $T$ | 绝对温度 (K) |
| $\frac{\partial U_{OCV}}{\partial T}$ | 熵系数 (V/K) |
| $R_{int}$ | 内阻 (Ω) |

### 4) 在SOC建模中的应用价值
- **热-电耦合**: 温度影响内阻和OCV，进而影响SOC估算精度
- **熵系数**: $\frac{\partial U_{OCV}}{\partial T}$ 随SOC变化，可用于SOC辨识
- **能量平衡**: 热生成反映电化学反应状态，关联SOC变化

**ANALYSIS RESULTS FOR: "What is keeping my phone awake? Characterizing and detecting no-sleep energy bugs in smartphone apps"**

Since I cannot read the PDF directly, I'll provide analysis based on this well-known paper (it's a seminal work in mobile energy bug research):

---

## 1) 智能手机能耗Bug类型 (Energy Bug Types)

This paper focuses specifically on **"No-Sleep Bugs"** - bugs that prevent the phone from entering low-power sleep state:

| Bug类型 | 描述 |
|---------|------|
| **Wakelock misuse** | 获取wakelock后未正确释放 |
| **Sensor listener leaks** | 传感器监听器未注销，持续唤醒设备 |
| **Infinite loop with wakelock** | 持有wakelock时进入无限循环 |
| **Exception-path wakelock leak** | 异常路径中wakelock未释放 |

---

## 2) 功耗测量方法 (Power Measurement Methods)

- **Power monitor hardware** (硬件功率计)
- **Battery drain rate analysis** (电池消耗速率分析)
- **Wakelock duration tracking** (唤醒锁持续时间追踪)

---

## 3) 检测工具 (Detection Tools)

**本文提出的工具**: 
- **开发了静态分析工具**检测no-sleep bugs
- 使用**数据流分析**追踪wakelock acquire/release配对

---

## 4) 节能建议 (Energy-Saving Recommendations)

1. 确保wakelock在所有执行路径（包括异常路径）正确释放
2. 使用timeout版本的wakelock作为安全保障
3. 及时注销不需要的sensor listeners
4. 避免在持有wakelock时执行长时间操作

---

Received April 7, 2020, accepted April 11, 2020, date of publication April 20, 2020, date of current version May 1, 2020. Digital Object Identifier 10.1109/ACCESS.2020.2988728
Evaluation of Li-Based Battery Current, Voltage, and Temperature Profiles for In-Service Mobile Phones
HAYDER ALI 1, (Student Member, IEEE), HASSAN ABBAS KHAN 1, (Member, IEEE), AND MICHAEL G. PECHT 2, (Life Fellow, IEEE)
1Department of Electrical Engineering, School of Science and Engineering, Lahore University of Management Sciences, Lahore 54792, Pakistan 2Center for Advanced Life Cycle Engineering (CALCE), University of Maryland, College Park, MD 20742, USA
Corresponding author: Hassan Abbas Khan (hassan.khan@lums.edu.pk) This work was supported by the Center for Advanced Life Cycle Engineering (CALCE) at the University of Maryland and Its over 150 Funding Companies.

ABSTRACT A battery's state of charge or runtime, and state of health or life, will depend on the product's discharge current over time. For a mobile phone, the discharge current depends on the specific apps that are operated. This paper presents an experimental study to measure and evaluate the operational charge/discharge profile, temperature and terminal voltage of six Android apps; WhatsApp, Facebook, Facebook Messenger, Instagram, Snapchat, and TikTok on smartphones. The results show how the discharge current required by an app's operation, will affect the battery runtime and life, due to the combined effect of discharge current and temperature.
INDEX TERMS Android apps, C-rates, in-service performance, Li batteries, smartphones.

I. INTRODUCTION The number of mobile (smartphone) users has surpassed 3.5 billion in 2020, which is 42% of the total global population [1], [2]. These users increasingly demand smartphones with improved functionality and performance to support gaming and social media applications (apps) [3]. This in turn stresses the battery system, which is widely considered as one of the weakest link in a modern mobile phone [4], [5]. In fact, some energy-intensive apps may reduce the battery operation time to as short as several hours for users [6], and were noted to be the likely cause of the Apple IOS slowdown problem in 2017 [7]. Krause et al. [8] showed the negative and highly non-linear impact of C-rate on the life of Li batteries. Dong et al. [9] also noted that accelerated battery aging occurred due to high discharge C-rates.
Reliability issues have also been highlighted with high C-rates as it causes high temperature which accelerates battery aging phenomena and at times causes thermal runaway [9], [10]. A significant drop in terminal voltage is also reported with high C-rates which impacts the smartphone operation [7]. Under high discharge C-rates, for a
The associate editor coordinating the review of this manuscript and
approving it for publication was Derek Abbott .

degraded battery with large internal resistance, heat dissipation becomes a major issue and unexpected smartphone shutdowns have been triggered even when there was substantial charge remaining in the battery [11]. This also results in lower effective battery capacity than the manufacturer's ratings.
Many efforts are made to address this problem of high C-rate in modern smartphones. For instance, some manufacturers suggest improving the algorithms for battery management system (BMS), but it can result in slowing down the operating system [7]. Another suggested method is to improve the system efficiency through low-power processors [5], [8]. Some researchers address the problem of high C-rate by improving battery capacity through improving the chemical operation of the battery by replacing the conventional electrodes of Li battery with the superior capacity (molybdenum disulfide) electrodes with enhanced functional capacity [12], [13].
With regards to in-use operation of smartphones, various studies suggests that traditional testing and qualification of Li-ion batteries may not be able to simulate the actual use of a smartphone in real environment [14], and that manufacturers must address the actual power dissipation of the apps as well as the batteries and smartphones circuitry [15], [16]. While some models have been developed

VOLUME 8, 2020 This work is licensed under a Creative Commons Attribution 4.0 License. For more information, see https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/

73665

H. Ali et al.: Evaluation of Li-Based Battery Current, Voltage, and Temperature Profiles

to profile power usage of smartphones, they rely on indirect methods to evaluate discharge patterns using aggregated current regimes [14], [17]�[20]. Direct measurement of discharge spike currents have also be conducted, but these rely on external sensors that can add significant measurement errors [17]�[19].
This paper presents the use of built-in mobile sensors to directly measure current discharge profile, terminal voltage, and battery temperature, when the phone is operated using six different apps: i.e., WhatsApp, Facebook, Facebook Messenger, Instagram, Snapchat, and TikTok. Our approach solves the problem of indirect measurements, and parameter averaging [21]. The proposed method uses the manufacturers' provided built-in mobile sensors data through an application program interface (API). We developed a relevant Android package kit (APK) to decipher battery parameters for the apps under investigation. We also provide the algorithm and API that we used (see Appendix) so other researchers can assess other mobile phones.
The rest of this paper is composed as follows. Section 2 summarizes Android-based phones and APIs that collect operation data. Section 3 presents discharge profile analyses of two case-study phones and discusses the impact of the six apps on battery drainage and temperature. Section 4 presents the conclusions.
II. ASSESSMENT OF SMARTPHONES FOR IN-SERVICE OPERATION The availability of in-service battery data, including current, voltage, and temperature, depends on manufacturer-provided software interfaces as well as the capability of the built-in sensor hardware in a smartphone. Higher resolution of data is key for accurate parameter assessment, for instance, a resolution of microamperes for peak current drawn is more useful as compared to milliampere or ampere level resolution. Similarly, the update time of parameters (minimum interval after which each value is updated in the software APIs) is also important to accurately gauge the parameter variations.
Seven Android-based smartphone manufacturers were evaluated1 using the developed APK to check the granularity (resolution and update time) of data for discharge current, terminal voltage, and battery temperature through the manufacturer-provided API (software). All the seven smartphone manufacturer support our developed APK, but most of the manufacturers do not provide high-granularity data to evaluate detailed current drawn from the batteries [22]. For instance, the current discharge profile for Samsung J7 is shown in Fig. 1(a), and no change in current was observed with varying app operation, although some variation in temperature was observed.
Similarly, a change in the current for Huawei Nexus P6 was averaged out for about 20 s, not allowing detailed information
1Note that Apple R is not in the list. The reason is that although most companies allow APIs to share data at some levels, Apple does not. With the release of iOS 10 in September 2016, Apple decided to completely hide this information from developers and end-users [7], [20].

of current spikes with a particular app operation. Further, the update of voltage or temperature is large and as a result no change was observed. In the case of a Google phone (Pixel 2), the current discharge profile and temperature/voltage profile averaged out after every 2 s and 15 s, respectively, as shown in Fig. 1(c). Like Samsung and Huawei, it also limits the resolution on current drainage values.
The results for all seven manufacturers are summarized in Table 1. Because the highest resolution of electrical current and the fastest update time was measured with the Vivo (V9) and Motorola (Droid Turbo), those two smartphones were selected for further study. Both unused phones under test were fully charged to compare against nameplate battery capacities to check for any unusual degradation, and both phones passed

the test. With regards to the market share, Vivo sold over 102 million phones worldwide in 2018 with a market share of about 7%, whereas Motorola sold over 35 million phones in 2018 [30].
Whereas a smartphone has numerous temperature sensors implemented across the device, battery temperature is typically sensed through a thermistor close to the terminals as shown for a typical assembly in Fig. 2 [31], [32]. In our developed app, the `EXTRA_TEMPERATURE' API command is used, which specifically measures the battery temperature [33]. III. RESULTS AND DISCUSSION To obtain the discharge profile, the Vivo (V9) and Motorola (Droid Turbo) phones were initially charged to full battery capacity (100%), and then each app was run for approximately 12-18 min, followed by the next charge cycle back to full charge. We found that 10+ min is sufficient to test all major functionalities in an app. Subsequently, the phone was discharged using the second app, and so on. During this time, most of the general capabilities of a specific app were tested. For example, in the case of WhatsApp, all features such as texting, photo send/download, voice note send/download, voice call, and video call were tested. The detailed features tested during the experiment for all apps are summarized in Table 2. Throughout the experiment, the experimental conditions were kept consistent to minimize variations from one app to the next. For instance, only the testing app (Snapchat or Facebook or others � only one at a time) along with our developed APK was run. All apps were tested on phones connected through WiFi and the sound volume and screen brightness were kept at 100% throughout the experiment ambient temperature at 26 � 0.5C.
For both phones under test, Fig. 3 shows the operation of various apps (i.e., WhatsApp, Facebook, Facebook Messenger, Instagram, Snapchat, and TikTok) on the current drain, the terminal voltage of the battery, and the battery temperature. The negative current represents charging, whereas the positive current shows discharging (Fig. 3(a)). Several positive spikes during charging appear which were triggered when the screen was turned on for viewing of the battery status. Terminal voltages (Fig. 3(b)) show an increasing trend while charging and a decreasing trend during the discharging, as expected. However, the rapid change observed in the current profile during charging is not seen in the voltage profile due to lower update time for the voltage (a software limitation
73667

H. Ali et al.: Evaluation of Li-Based Battery Current, Voltage, and Temperature Profiles

FIGURE 3. Charging/discharging profile of Vivo V9 and Motorola Droid for (a) current profile, (b) battery terminal voltage profile, and (c) battery temperature profile.

TABLE 2. Units features tested during experiment for all apps properties.
by the manufacturer). Each sensor has a separate resolution and update times as provided by the manufacturer and summarized in Table 1 where, for most of the manufacturers, the update time of the voltage sensor is high (several seconds) as compared to the current sensor (milliseconds).
The result in Fig. 3(a) show that the discharge rate depends on the apps and is also dictated by mobile phone technology (e.g., processor chipset, camera technology, screen type, and resolution). For instance, the processor used in Vivo V9 (Qualcomm MSM8953-Pro Snapdragon 626) is more energy-efficient than the one used in Motorola Droid Turbo (Qualcomm APQ8084 Snapdragon 805) due to improved process technology node from 28 nm (Motorola) to 14 nm (Vivo) [27], [28]. Further, the screen resolution for Motorola is 565 ppi in comparison with 400 ppi for Vivo. A higher
73668

resolution requires more pixel per inch and ultimately consumes more energy per inch. So, the behavior of apps vary significantly on various phones and ultimately the same app can perform in a varied manner on different mobile manufacturers [27], [28].
The temperature profile for both phones is shown in Fig. 3(c). The temperature consistently increases during app usage and decreases during the charging process (in comparison with the discharge operation under app usage) [35], [36]. For the Vivo V9 phone, the temperature is fairly consistent during the use of various apps and, during the charging interval, the temperature normalizes to below 32 C with the highest battery temperature of about 40 C. However, for the Motorola phone, usage of Snapchat, TikTok, and Facebook Messenger particularly increases the temperature beyond 43 C and at least 3 C higher than all other app usage or charging conditions. The amount of current spikes from the battery is also higher for Snapchat, TikTok, and Facebook Messenger for the Motorola phone, which could significantly affect aging and other related issues [37], [38]. To describe the current discharge profile, the mean current information is not sufficient to characterize these apps. In addition, current spike information is also essential as it can have many unintended consequences such as temperature rise and lifetime deterioration.
In order to evaluate this further, we calculate the probability density functions (PDFs) and normal distribution functions (NDFs) of the discharge current profiles for all the apps
VOLUME 8, 2020

H. Ali et al.: Evaluation of Li-Based Battery Current, Voltage, and Temperature Profiles

FIGURE 4. Probability density functions (PDFs) and normal distribution functions (NDFs) for all apps for the battery discharge cycle.

under investigation in Fig. 4. The PDFs/NDFs facilitates the reader to understand and reconstruct the results for further studies.
A PDF specifies the probability distribution for various levels of current drawn while using various apps. The highest point on the PDF relates to the mean value of current. In the case of the Motorola phone, several apps such as Snapchat have a higher mean value (967 mA) compared to Instagram (572 mA) with results summarized in Table 3. Higher currents drawn from the battery are undesirable from a heat dissipation perspective. In addition, high-frequency current spikes are not suitable for optimum battery operation as they rapidly degrade battery life [37], [39]. The values of standard deviation (SD) along with mean currents is also summarized in Table 3 for reconstruction of discharge profiles.
Experiments showed that the discharge profile for the six apps is significantly different on each phone. With regards to C-rate discharge on phones, higher rates are detrimental for both battery runtime and lifetime. Typically, up to 0.3C is considered a safe limit for accelerated lithium-ion battery degradation [40]�[42]. Therefore, the probability of
VOLUME 8, 2020

TABLE 3. Summary of mean current drawn and probability of current spikes above 0.3C for Vivo and Motorola phones.
current spikes above 0.3C is shown in Table 3 to assess the power usage of various apps under consideration. It can be noticed that the performance of energy-intensive apps such as Facebook Messenger and Snapchat vary from Vivo to Motorola. For instance, in the case of Vivo, the mean
73669

H. Ali et al.: Evaluation of Li-Based Battery Current, Voltage, and Temperature Profiles

FIGURE 5. Cumulative probability function for all apps under test. The y-axis shows the probability of not exceeding a current threshold on the x-axis.
current and the probability of current spikes above 0.3C for Facebook Messenger are 740 mA and 16.7%, respectively, which is higher than Snapchat with a mean current and probability of current spikes at 705 mA and 2.8%, respectively. Further, higher current spikes may not necessarily result in higher mean currents as seen with Snapchat, which has a mean current of 705 mA with a current spike probability of 2.8% compared with WhatsApp with a mean current of 653 mA and a current spike probability of 5.8%. Higher current spikes are associated with several well-documented issues such as degradation, capacity fade, and premature shutdowns [43]�[45].
With regards to Motorola, the performance of Facebook Messenger is relatively better than Snapchat as well as TikTok. The mean current for Facebook Messenger is 845 mA, compared to 967 mA for Snapchat and 933 mA for TikTok. Similarly, the current spike probability for Facebook Messenger is lower at 17.2% compared to 21.2% for Snapchat and 20.1% for TikTok. Fig. 5 plots the cumulative probability function (CPF) to link the current discharge profile with the probability of occurrence. The CPF also shows a variation in amplitude of the current spikes for all the apps. A spread across the x-axis (current) shows a higher probability of occurrence for a larger spike, which is undesirable. It is therefore evident from Fig. 5 that the performance of the Vivo
73670

V9 is better than the Motorola Droid Turbo for the use of apps under consideration.
With regards to the battery capacity and runtime of smartphones, power-hungry apps could reduce the operation time significantly. For instance, for the Motorola Droid Turbo model with a 3900 mAh battery [28], talk time of up to 48 h is suggested by the manufacturer, whereas our data shows that using Snapchat and TikTok for less than 5 h would fully drain the battery. On the other hand, using Instagram for at least 7 h is possible with the in-use discharge pattern. In addition to the average current drawn, C-rating is another critical parameter that plays an essential role in battery life span [41]. Typically, a C-rating of 0.1C to 0.4C for lithium-ion batteries is recommended by many manufacturers [42], [46], [47]. So, it is important to optimize app development to limit the battery current spikes. The C-rating of batteries for the phones investigated in this paper is not specified by the manufacturers, but our analysis shows that for the Motorola phone, current spikes of over 0.6C were observed, whereas the maximum current spikes for the Vivo phone were less than 0.5C. Therefore, manufacturers should provide this criterion while specifying the usage time of the phones under continuous operation.
IV. CONCLUSION This study monitored and assessed the in-service charge/ discharge profile (e.g. current discharge, terminal voltage, and battery temperature) of Android-based smartphones during active operation of the six most downloaded social media apps of 2019, i.e., WhatsApp, Facebook, Facebook Messenger, Instagram, Snapchat, and TikTok. The in-service charge/discharge profile was retrieved through our developed Android package kit (APK), which accesses the internal sensors through the manufacturer-provided application program interface (API). The interested readers can see our APP program in the Appendix.
As was demonstrated in this study, the discharge rate depended on the apps and their operation, as well as the phone hardware. For instance, the processor used in Vivo V9 (Qualcomm MSM8953-Pro Snapdragon 626) appears to be more energy efficient than the one used in Motorola Droid Turbo (Qualcomm APQ8084 Snapdragon 805). As a result the same app draws less current on Vivo phone as compare to Motorola phone.
This study also found that the operation of an app, for any given phone, will affect its long-term performance and reliability. For instance, Snapchat operation on Motorola was found to be the most energy and temperature intensive with a mean discharge current of 967 mA (for usage interval of approx. 20 min) with temperature rising to 45 C. The apps Instagram, Facebook, WhatsApp, Facebook Messenger and TikTok showed lower mean discharge currents of 572, 610, 653, 854 and 933 mA, respectively during operation.
In addition to mean discharge current, electrical current spikes in current drawn is useful in modeling battery degradation through accelerated aging processes. For the Motorola phone, results showed that the probability
VOLUME 8, 2020

H. Ali et al.: Evaluation of Li-Based Battery Current, Voltage, and Temperature Profiles

of current spikes beyond 0.3C in Snapchat and TikTok is more than 20%, which is significantly higher than all other apps. For the Vivo phone, the operation of Facebook Messenger was significantly more energy-intensive compared to Snapchat or TikTok. Higher spikes in current results in non-linear temperature rise due to I2R dissipation resulting in accelerated aging process [37], [38].
While manufacturers suggest a runtime for smartphone batteries (the time the battery lasts in one full charge), our findings from in-service phone operation confirm that the actual (experimental) runtime is significantly lower than manufacturer-suggested talk time or operation time. In particular, the discharge currents are underestimated for in-service operation resulting in shortened operational duration. Taking the operation of Snapchat on a Motorola phone as an example, the mean discharge current is 967 mA (approx. C/4 rate � i.e., runtime to about 4 h), which is significantly smaller than the manufacturer-suggested phone runtime of up to 48 h. While the phrase ``up to 48 hours'' can be the highest limit, it is misleading for many consumers, since the actual runtime will be an order of magnitude lower for Snapchat operation. Similarly, the mean discharge current for Snapchat for Vivo V9 was 705 mA (approx. C/5 rate) with runtime of about 5 hr.
Finally, the information presented in this paper evaluates the discharge profiles for smartphones under different app usage for only two manufacturers. In the future, other phones could be considered. However, not all smartphone manufacturers allow/support measurement of discharge profile in high resolution (milli-second). For instance, Apple does not provide the relevant API, whereas Samsung and Google provides only an average current drainage profile [7], [48]. The average value provided by these manufacturers does not contain information on spike currents or effect on battery drainage by various app features on short time intervals, which are essential to characterize the operation of apps or model degradation based on varying consumer behavior.

requirement for LIBs utilized in smartphones and other portable electronic devices is the capability of fast-charging, without compromising cycle life. While modifications to the cathode material and cell design can improve the fast-charging capability,[21] the most efficient utilization of LIBs can be provided by Battery Management Systems (BMS) based on electrochemical models reflecting physical states inside the battery.[22�25]
BMS utilize a mathematical model of a battery to control its operation and typically refer to a hardware and software-based solution, where the software usually handles tasks such as the estimation of the state-of-charge (SOC) and state-of-health (SOH), charging algorithm, and establishing safe operating constraints. Models for BMS can range from empirical equivalent circuit based models to physics-based electrochemical models.[26] Empirical models have milder memory and computational requirements than physics-based models but do not provide insights into physical internal states of the battery that determine its performance and aging characteristics. Memory and computational capability of the hardware for the target applications typically determine the type of models used in the BMS. We discuss two potential barriers to the introduction of physics-based electrochemical model BMS in the area of consumer electronics applications for determining online fastcharging strategies and how they can be overcome.
1) Algorithms for parameter estimation for the electrochemical model: The performance of BMS in terms of accurate SOC/ SOH estimation, power prediction, and online strategies for fast-charging is directly related to the quality of the initial model and the adaptation of model parameters as the battery ages. However, validating results of algorithms that estimate electrochemical model parameters is a time consuming process that requires specific experimental expertise.
2) Resource constraints: Algorithms for state and parameter estimation based on electrochemical models can be resource (power) intensive for real-time implementation in consumer electronics. Such models typically involve coupled nonlinear Partial Differential Equations that are solved by model order reduction techniques.[27] Despite having enough computation power, most consumer electronics still run on less resource intensive empirical models in order to preserve power for user applications.
2. Standalone Electrochemical Model
In order to get the full benefit of using physics-based electrochemical model BMS for fast-charging strategies, it is imperative to adapt the model parameters as the battery ages. The development, implementation, and validation of such algorithms are challenging. Generally, given a fully parameterized physics-based electrochemical model, i.e., containing all relevant electrolyte transport properties and electrode parameters, for a specific battery at a given age, an optimal charging profile that maximizes input charge current to the battery to reduce charging time can be derived. Such a charging profile is constrained to current, voltage, and temperature limits specified by the cell manufacturer while minimizing side reactions and other degradation mechanisms that could potentially age

the battery faster such as lithium plating, electrolyte decomposition, and active material dissolution. However, such an approach is resource intensive. As an alternative we propose a standalone solution. The proposed approach maintains the same benefits as a physics-based electrochemical model for fast-charging applications, while utilizing simpler building blocks for charging profile generation and adaption rules that guarantee safe and efficient operation of the battery during charging, throughout the lifetime of the battery.[28] We use the following approach:
1) Given a specific battery, we parameterize electrochemical models at beginning-of-life (BoL), end-of-life (EoL), and at different stages in-between, through accelerated aging tests under operating conditions of interest.
2) Subsequently, for each aged state we optimize the charging strategies, i.e., minimize charging time, while reducing degradation at that age.
3) The developed charging strategies at each stage in the battery life are then approximated using simple building blocks such as constant current (CC), constant voltage (CV), and current ramps.
4) In a final step, adaptation rules and switching criteria are then developed to identify charging strategies at any age of the battery. Here, switching criteria refer to conditions based on SOC and SOH and are meant to trigger changes between different building blocks such as between CC to CV or CC to ramp current to CV and so forth.[29]
With this approach, we utilize the physical insights from electrochemical models, but use a simpler approach for real time implementation where only the building blocks and adaptation rules are required in addition to the estimate of the battery SOH for optimized battery charging.
The implementation of such a standalone electrochemical model-based charging strategy is demonstrated for the LG-G5 smartphone battery on an external battery cycler. We compare the impact of an Original Equipment Manufacturer (OEM)specified fast-charging protocol and an electrochemical modelbased fast-charging protocol on the capacity fade, swelling, and structural integrity of the active materials in anode and cathode. Using a complementary set of experimental characterization methods ranging from synchrotron in operando to lab-scale techniques, we show not only the functionality of an electrochemical model-based fast-charging strategy in consumer electronics but also its ability to suppress lithium plating. Finally, to the best of our knowledge, for the first time, we show how lithium depositions on the anode lead to inactivated cathode regions with a lower lithiation grade.
3. Performance Comparison of Cycling Protocols
The charging protocols considered include the OEM-specified fast-charging protocol (hereafter OEMcyc) and a fast-charging protocol derived using a physics-based model (hereafter MODcyc) of the LG-G5 (BL-42D1F) LIB (for cell specifications see the Experimental Section); it is important to note here that "fast-charging" refers to a charging time of < 2 h. Details of the
